*trained* slot the model's own conditioning explains its images well, while for **any** OOD query the
freshly composed ADDRESS conditioning explains the query worse than that. So 80 % of genuine
new-*combinations* exceed the bar too. The verification cannot separate "ADDRESS is imperfect because
the composition is new" from "ADDRESS is imperfect because the vocabulary is missing", which is
exactly the distinction it was introduced to make.

*(This was measured three times before it was measured correctly. `route_signals` returns `s_novel`
as a minimum over the **class** conditionings only — anything passed as `basis_conds` is reported
separately as `basis_*` — so reading `s_novel` after supplying an ADDRESS conditioning measures a
quantity that conditioning never enters. The first two runs were reading the wrong key and produced
`t_self` byte-identical to `t_novel`. The corrected run above reproduces the same verdict, so the
conclusion stands, but the earlier numbers measured my mistake.)*

**What is established, and where this line stops.** The over-spend is a *calibration* limit, not a
detection limit: the detector separates at AUROC 0.995 and a zero-error threshold provably exists in
(0.01758, 0.01813). Five label-free routes to it are now closed, four of them for one generalizable
reason — **the positive class of this decision cannot be produced by the system that has to make it**,
whether by synthesising conditionings, hiding a known value, rescaling for self-sample bias, or
checking the cheap route after the fact. The current calibration is a deliberate corner of a real
trade (§6c: never under-spend), not an oversight, and nothing measured here justifies moving it.

What would open it is a source of genuine out-of-vocabulary examples that does not come from the
generator. In a lifelong setting the **stream** is exactly that, but using it requires a verification
signal that works — and the one tried above does not, so online recalibration needs a stronger
outcome check (e.g. whether the *consolidated* concept reproduces its query) rather than a residual
denoising error. Recorded as the next thing to try, not as a plan that is expected to work.

## 20c. F9 was rendered at an operating point no table used (2026-08-13)

The ColorMNIST case-study panels looked broken -- the `ours` row scoring 0.33 / 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00
across the four blocks while `poe-guided` and `query-only` returned clean digits -- and the obvious
suspect was the prototype-distance dial. It was not the dial.

`make_case_studies.py` defaults `--guidance 1.5`, and its own help says why: that is what n9's
label-free calibration selects on this backbone, and *"the figure must use the same value the table
does"*. `reproduce.sh` was overriding it to **2.0**. Same code, same dial, same backbone:

| dial | guidance | `ours` joint | `ours` recall |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.9 | **2.0** — what f9 was rendered at | **0.083** | 0.980 |
| 0.9 | **1.5** — what every quoted number was measured at | **0.583** | 0.854 |

A **7× accuracy collapse** from a flag in the build script. The figure and the tables were being
produced at different operating points, and the figure was the one that moved. Re-rendered at the
default, the `ours` row goes to **1.00 / 0.17 / 0.50 / 0.67** at recall 0.73–0.99. The override is
removed from `reproduce.sh` and the interaction is recorded in the `--dial` help so it cannot recur.

**The dial itself was re-confirmed, and the tempting move is the wrong one.** Swept under current
code at the corrected guidance:

| dial | `ours` joint | `ours` recall | harmonic | per-block recall in f9 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.7 | **0.833** | **0.116** | 0.204 | — |
| **0.8 (default since 2026-08-13)** | 0.792 | 0.427 | 0.555 | 0.22 / 0.57 / **0.00** / 0.92 |
| 0.9 (metric-optimal) | 0.583 | **0.854** | **0.693** | 0.73 / 0.99 / 0.74 / 0.96 |
| *`poe-guided` / `query-only`* | *0.958* | ***0.000*** | *0.000* | *0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.00* |

**The default is 0.8, and it was chosen on the renders rather than on this table.** It also runs
against §20b, which swept this dial at **3 seeds** and put s=0.8 more than **four standard errors**
below s=0.90 (F1 0.410 ± 0.058 against 0.669 ± 0.018) — 0.8 had in fact been the provisional default
once already and was superseded on that evidence. The metric prefers
0.9 on the combined axis (harmonic 0.693 against 0.555) and on coverage (0.854 against 0.427); 0.8 was
selected for colour faithfulness in the case-study panels, which is a legitimate criterion for a
*figure* whose job is to show what the arm produces. The cost is recorded here rather than absorbed:
coverage roughly halves, and in one of the four displayed blocks it goes to **0.000** — the same
number the two memorizing baselines score there.

**Nothing in the tables moves with it.** The dial affects only the `ours` row of f9; every composition
number quoted anywhere else (5e-ter, 5e-bis, 5e-sexies, N49) is measured without it, at the sampler's
own settings. So this is a presentation choice with a known price, not a change to a result.

**Do not read it as an operating point.** 0.7 was also swept and is where the trade becomes
self-defeating: joint rises to 0.833 while recall falls to **0.116**, barely above the 0.000 scored by
the arms that get their accuracy by re-emitting the query. The panels look cleanest exactly where the
arm has stopped doing the thing it is claimed to do, because sampling near the prototype *is* what
accuracy-without-coverage looks like, and coverage at accuracy is the entire claim (5e-ter,
5e-sexies). The generalizable point: a render cannot select an operating point for an arm whose
selling point is coverage — the failure mode that most improves appearance is the one that concedes
the argument. Both columns, or neither.

## 5s-bis. C2: iterative concept selection does not beat one greedy pass (2026-08-13)

The second stated contribution was to *iteratively* select concepts rather than take K from a pool
grown once. The motivation is in `candidates_ascent`'s own docstring — "submodular selection can only
choose from the pool ascent found, and a thin pool gives a bad choice" — and it is visible in the case
studies, where selected prototypes are often unrelated to the query. The paper's answer is a 20×
bigger pool (their Table 6 grows 1920 chains). `compose.iterative_teacher` interleaves instead: pick
one prototype, compute what the partial teacher still fails to explain, grow the next chains from
there.

**Budget-matched, it does nothing.** 8 held-out ColorMNIST compositions, 24 ascent chains for *every*
arm (K rounds of 24/K for the iterative ones, so total compute is equal):

| arm | joint | digit | fg | **bg** | pool |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `one-shot` — the paper's Eq. 8–9 route | **0.664** | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.664 | 103 |
| `iter-residual` — regrow from what is unexplained | 0.656 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.656 | 117 |
| `iter-teacher` — regrow from the partial teacher | 0.656 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.656 | 115 |
| `iter-query` — regrow from the query (control) | 0.656 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.656 | 117 |

−0.008 against one-shot, **0.04 pooled standard errors**. Not distinguishable, and not close to it.

**The informative part is that all three iterative variants are identical to each other**, to three
decimals, on all eight compositions. `iter-query` regrows without conditioning on the selection at
all — it is the control for "iterating helps" versus "restarts help" — and it lands on exactly the
same number as the two that do condition. So the *direction* of regrowth is not weak; it is inert.

**Why, and it relocates the bottleneck.** Look at the per-factor columns: `digit` and `fg` are
**1.000 for every arm**, and every point of the difference sits in `bg` (0.656–0.664). The teacher is
not failing because the pool lacks the right prototype — it finds digit and foreground perfectly from
a pool of ~100. It fails on background, which §5q already identifies as the factor the backbone
*under-responds* to (background vectors have mean norm 0.55 against 0.79 for digit, which is why
`backbone_gains` exists). Enlarging the pool from 103 to 117 candidates, or aiming it better, cannot
fix a factor the model represents weakly. **That also predicts the paper's own remedy would not fix
this error**: 1920 chains would search the same under-powered background direction 20× harder.

**Two limits on how far to read this.**

* *Low power.* 8 compositions, s.e. 0.134 — this test could only detect an effect of about ±0.27.
  What it rules out is a large effect, not a small one. The flatness across three variants is the
  stronger evidence, because it does not depend on the error bar.
* *Scored through THEIR Stage 4.* Samples come from `sample_teacher`, i.e. the paper's Eq. 13, which
  is deterministic and collapses — hence `recall 0.000` for every arm including the baseline. Our own
  Stage 4 reads μ_T into `c_new` and samples the model's conditional, so a selection change reaches
  our pipeline by a different route. The teacher is what C2 modifies and its composition accuracy is
  the direct measure, but a null here does not strictly transfer to our sampler.

**Status: C2 is implemented and does not pay for itself.** `iterative_teacher` is kept — it is the
honest home for `regrow_from`, and the diagnostic is the record — but the contribution as stated is
not supported, and the outline should say so rather than list it as a feature.

## 6e-bis. Why composition is strong and the representation is not: they are the same fact

§6e reads as a tension with §3 and §5q. ADDRESS composes held-out combinations well — F1 0.630 our
way, 0.756 for a basis direction, joint 0.556 — and yet twelve consolidated concepts leave the
representation **indistinguishable from no adaptation at all** (linear digit 0.271 against 0.266,
oracle 0.406). Twelve extra conditioning columns buy nothing.

They are not in tension. They are one property seen twice.

**ADDRESS adds a POINT, never a DIRECTION.** `c_new = μ + Σ_f gain_f · V_f[value]`, and μ and every
V_f are least-squares fits to the 120 *seen* embeddings. So `c_new` is in the affine span of what the
backbone already had, by construction. Measured (`tests/diag_span_vs_representation.py`), relative
residual against that span:

| | median | mean |
|---|---|---|
| **ADDRESS `c_new`** | **0.0000** | 0.0000 |
| the ORACLE's *trained* embedding for the same held-out class | **0.7359** | 0.7340 |
| *a seen embedding against the other 119 — the scale bar* | *0.2016* | *0.2062* |

The scale bar matters: the span is not trivially everything, since a genuine trained embedding sits
0.20 outside the span of its own peers. The oracle's held-out embeddings sit at **0.736**, more than
three times further out. ADDRESS sits at **zero**.

**That explains both results at once, and the cost too.**

* *Composition works* because placing a concept correctly requires only a point in the right place,
  and the span already contains it. That is precisely what "the factor basis spans it" means.
* *The representation cannot improve* because a readout over conditionings gains no new axis — the
  twelve new columns are functions of the 120 old ones. There is nothing for a probe to exploit.
* *72 bytes* is the same statement in units of storage. 72 bytes is what it costs to **name a point
  in a space you already have**. Cheapness and representational inertness are two readings of one
  fact, not a trade that happened to come out well.

So §6e is not a weakness of the method; it is the tier behaving exactly as defined. What would be a
weakness is claiming ADDRESS enriches a representation, and nothing here does.

**A correction to how this section first read.** It said N50 "tested only the ADDRESS tier". That is
wrong: `learn_concept_factored` pins the embedding to `c_new` and then calls `consolidate`, which
trains a **LoRA adapter** per concept. N50's `ours` is ADDRESS *plus* an adapter. What is true, and
is the actual defect, is that N50's **readout never activates those adapters** — `conditional_losses`
substitutes an embedding, and an adapter only applies inside `bank.use(name)`. So the span argument
above explains precisely what N50 measured (embedding columns, all in-span), and N50 did not measure
what it claimed to.

**The prediction this makes, and it is falsifiable.** If the representation is carried by the
*embedding* alone, it cannot improve — the span result says so exactly. If it is carried by the
**adapter**, it can, because a LoRA changes the function from (x_t, u, c) to epsilon and is not
confined to any span. Absorbing a concept trains one. So the corrected experiment (N53) should show
`ours` above `base` when each concept's adapter is opened for its own column, and the size of that
gap is the real answer to "are the representations strong". EXTEND BASIS, which fits a genuinely new
direction, is the third point on the same axis.

## 5t-bis. Shapes3D: four from-scratch recipes fail, and what that isolates

Shapes3D is registered against `lifelong_ood/data/factored.py`'s contract -- 480,000 images, six
factors as an exact Cartesian product, split into composition and nuisance because a full 6-tuple has
exactly ONE image and every coverage metric would otherwise be undefined.  `test_factored` asserts
the split starves no factor value, and `tests/diag_shapes3d_probes.py` verifies the instrument before
anything is scored with it: floor_hue **0.999**, object_hue **1.000**, shape **0.973** against base
rates 0.130 / 0.114 / 0.260.

**Then four attempts to train a conditional backbone on it all failed the same way**: excellent
samples, ignored label.

| recipe | seen-slot joint | note |
|---|---|---|
| 300 compositions, 40 images each, 60 epochs | **0.000** | classes too rare |
| 100 compositions, 200 images each, 200 epochs | 0.070 | ColorMNIST's exact density |
| stage-2 conditioning fine-tune from (2) | 0.062 | "learn images first" -- no gain |
| `wall_hue` pinned, from scratch | **0.062** | within-class variance -- no gain |

*chance is 0.010; ColorMNIST reaches ~0.86 on seen slots.*

**The diagnosis, and it is not sample quality.**  `tests/diag_conditioning_gap.py` measures
`loss(wrong class) - loss(right class)` on TRAINING images, plus the null gap:

| backbone | gap | **null gap** |
|---|---|---|
| ColorMNIST (works) | +0.00140 | **+0.00018** |
| nobg3 (works) | +0.00104 | **+0.00008** |
| **Shapes3D from scratch** | +0.00082 | **−0.00088** |

A negative null gap means the CFG null token explains training images **better than their own class
embedding** -- the class channel is net-harmful, so the model does best ignoring it.  On a marginal
as easy as flat-shaded rendered scenes the epsilon loss is nearly minimised without ever consulting
the label.

*(An earlier reading of mine, that a near-zero pairwise cosine between class embeddings showed they
never differentiated, was wrong: ColorMNIST's is −0.0039 and it works.  Near-zero cosine is what
256-dimensional embeddings do.  The null gap is the diagnostic; the cosine is not.)*

**Two hypotheses were tested and both refuted.**  "Learn the domain first, then conditioning" (row 3)
and "within-class variance swamps the label" (row 4) each gave 0.062.  So the failure is not
explained by training order or by nuisance variance, and the remaining difference in N57 is the one
that matters: **starting from a backbone that was pretrained on this domain**, which is exactly what
made CelebA work (§29d: from-scratch conditional training at 128 px never learned conditioning
either, and N47's fine-tune did).

`n57` + `n58` run that route on `eurecom-ds/scoresdeve-ema-shapes3d-64`, bridged VE->VP by
`neural/ve_bridge.py`.  **The conditioning gap is reported FIRST in N58**, because everything below it
is meaningless if the label is still decorative -- which is the mistake these four rows exist to stop
anyone repeating.

## 7z. Is the architecture UNet-specific?  A latent DiT says no (N55/N56, figures/dit/)

Everything up to here runs on a pixel-space UNet whose class embedding is added to the time
embedding.  The three-tier scheme is defined entirely over a **class embedding table** -- `factorize`
fits `c ~ mu + sum_f V_f[value]` to it, ADDRESS composes a point in that basis, the router reads
epsilon-error under candidate conditionings -- and none of that mentions convolutions.  If the scheme
is architectural rather than incidental it should survive being moved onto a transformer that
conditions through adaLN modulation and works on latent patches, which is the family
vision-language-action models are built from.

**The test is the reuse, not the reimplementation.**  `neural/dit.py` exposes the UNet's exact
interface -- `cemb` table with a null row, and `emb_override` -- so `factorize` and `compose` run on
it **unchanged**.  Anything that had needed a DiT-specific path would have marked a place the
architecture was secretly UNet-specific.  Nothing did.

| | pixel UNet | **latent DiT** |
|---|---|---|
| basis R^2 on the class table | 0.870 | **0.832** |
| params / training | ~1 h | 7.5M, **6.8 min** |
| conditioning path | class embedding added to the time embedding | adaLN-Zero off the same sum |

The basis emerges just as strongly, and R^2 is a genuine like-for-like number: the same `factorize`
call on the same kind of table.

**The full loop, and a correction.**  N55 first reported "ADDRESS on the DiT, joint 0.807".  That
number composed from factor values decoded from the held-out SLOT ID -- the ground truth.  It answers
*can the basis express this composition*, not *can the system work out which composition is needed*;
discovery was skipped, and reporting it alone overstates the pipeline.  N56 runs the loop the UNet
actually runs (`learn_factored`, one softmax per factor, optimised so the composed conditioning
explains the query):

| | value |
|---|---|
| READ accuracy from **one query, no labels** | digit 0.917, fg 1.000, bg 1.000, **all three 0.917** |
| **FULL-LOOP joint** | **0.661** |
| ORACLE joint (ground-truth values) | 0.859 |
| **what discovery costs** | **0.198** |

So 18 parameters determined from a single query recover the composition 92 % of the time, and the
honest end-to-end number is 0.661.

**The autoencoder is accepted on PROBES, not MSE**, and that mattered twice.  A first fit reached
mse 0.00044 -- excellent -- while the foreground probe fell 1.000 -> 0.835, because the digit stroke
is a few hundred pixels against ~2800 of background and per-pixel MSE barely sees it.  A second fit,
with foreground weighting, diverged to predicting black; the weighting had scaled the loss 8x without
renormalising, which is an 8x learning-rate change in disguise.  The accepted AE is mse 0.00020 with
a worst factor drop of +0.002.  Both guards are kept and they catch different failures: one finds
"trained fine, lost a factor", the other finds "did not train at all".

**Held out from the autoencoder too.**  The AE is fit on `X[seen]` only -- 24 000 of 32 000 images,
120 compositions -- so the 40 held-out compositions never reached it.  Their latents come from
encoding with a model that never trained on them.

**What this does and does not license.**  It licenses "the three-tier scheme is a property of having
a class-embedding table, not of the convolutional backbone", which is the claim that has to hold for
this to reach VLA models.  It does not license a DiT-beats-UNet comparison: the two are measured on
different sampling paths, and `figures/dit/d4_unet_vs_dit.png` is a like-for-like *render* rather than
a matched protocol.

*Open, and visible in `figures/dit/d5_dit_prototypes.png`:* latent mode ascent recovers clean modes
from query-initialised chains, but the RANDOM-initialised chains collapse to the same two attractors
for every composition.  That is a defect -- 5e-quinquies leans on random-init chains as the control
that never sees the query -- and its likeliest cause is that the diagnostic seeds one generator per
call, so every block gets identical random starts.

## 6c-ter. A sixth attempt on the router: description length instead of a threshold (2026-08-13)

**Verdict first, and it changed once the measurement went deep enough.** At the benchmark's own
operating point — one query per concept — description-length routing is catastrophically worse than
the threshold cascade (0.333 against 0.833). At **N = 32 and above it becomes indistinguishable from
it** (0.722 / 0.778 against 0.833 — on 18 concepts that is 14 or 15 of 18, a one-concept gap), while
using **no calibrated threshold anywhere**. The cascade is still nominally ahead at every N tested and
is not being replaced. What changed is that the rule is no longer a failure; it is a rule that needs
evidence the benchmark does not supply, and the amount it needs was predicted in advance by its own
arithmetic.

*The first version of this section concluded "it loses at every batch size, and it is not close." That
was measured over N ≤ 16 — and 6c-ter's own exchange rate says promotions need ~15 and ~36 instances,
so the sweep stopped exactly where the effect starts. `tests/diag_mdl_stream.py` caught the
truncation; the deep run (N = 8/16/32/64, `results/diag_mdl_router_deep.json`) is what settles it.*

**The reframing.** Every attempt in 6c-bis asks a classification question — "IS this query out of
vocabulary?" — and calibrates a threshold to answer it. Cobweb never asks that. Presented with an
instance it does not classify it as novel-or-not; it applies each operator hypothetically and scores
the resulting partitions by category utility, an objective with a complexity penalty built in. No
threshold, no positive class, only a comparison between futures of the same instance. The storage
ladder supplies that penalty for free, because the tiers already have honest costs in bits:

    L(tier) = C_tier + N * (bits to code one example under that tier)          (*)
    NOTHING 0 b   ADDRESS 576 b   EXTEND BASIS 8192 b   BLOCK 2.2 Mb

Two consequences motivated trying it. First, `route_by_trial`'s confound **cancels**: it failed
because for *any* OOD query the composed ADDRESS conditioning explains the query worse than a trained
slot explains its own images, so 80 % of genuine new-combinations tripped the bar — but that term is
common to both sides of a difference and cannot affect which tier is smaller. Second, **N and the
bits-per-residual constant enter (*) only as a product**, so the batch-size sweep *is* the calibration
sensitivity sweep and the ordering of crossover points is calibration-free.

**The codelength had to be the real one.** The first version used the simple loss as a Gaussian
codelength and routed every query to "store nothing". That is not a tuning failure: the simple loss
IS the variational bound with its per-timestep weights divided out (Ho et al. §3.4 drop them to
improve sample quality), so it under-counts evidence by orders of magnitude. `router.elbo_bits`
computes the eps-matching part of the bound instead; for a difference between two conditionings on
the same data the prior, reconstruction and entropy terms all cancel.

*It is measurable, which was not obvious.* The bound puts half its weight above u = 0.64 and its five
heaviest grid points at u = 0.98, 0.97, 0.02, 0.96, 0.03 — where x_t is nearly pure noise and the
conditioning barely enters. `tests/diag_elbo_stability.py` was written to check whether the rule was
reading its own sampling noise. It is not: with common random numbers across tiers, |mean|/sd is
**17.3 at two draws and 93.6 at eight** on the hardest case (an in-distribution query, smallest true
difference), sign stable across all six seeds. But the sign is **not stable across bands** — on a
new-vocabulary query the full bound says ADDRESS codes 17.2 ± 1.5 bits *worse* while u ∈ [0.10, 0.70]
says 7.6 ± 0.8 bits *better*. Both are stable and they disagree; the full bound is the principled
choice and its answer is the semantically right one, but any claim about the sign must name its band.

**The measured result.** Accuracy against the number of instances the concept has arrived as
(18 concepts, `results/diag_mdl_router_deep.json`; the cascade is batch-blind by construction — it
decides on the first instance and never revisits — so its row is flat by definition):

| rule | N=8 | N=16 | **N=32** | **N=64** | overspend @64 | underspend @64 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`cascade` (6c, what runs today)** | **0.833** | **0.833** | **0.833** | **0.833** | 0.00 | 0.25 |
| `mdl` — equation (*) | 0.333 | 0.333 | **0.722** | **0.778** | 0.08 | 0.25 |
| `crossover` — same rule, read as N* | 0.333 | 0.333 | **0.778** | 0.778 | 0.25 | 0.08 |
| `utility` — category utility, literally | 0.611 | 0.611 | 0.778 | 0.722 | 0.25 | 0.17 |
| `sign` — is the coding gain positive? | 0.389 | 0.389 | 0.611 | 0.556 | 0.67 | 0.00 |

`mdl` climbs 0.333 → 0.778 as evidence accumulates and lands one concept short of the cascade, having
matched its under-spend (0.25) at a small over-spend (0.08). `sign`, which drops the exchange rate
entirely, over-spends on two thirds of queries at every depth and is the one variant that is simply
wrong.

**The promotion curve is the actual result**, because it is the part a threshold cannot produce at
all. Mean ladder position, same concepts at every N:

| truth | N=8 | N=16 | N=32 | N=64 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| in-distribution | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.00 | **0.00** |
| new-combination | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.83 | **1.00** |
| new-vocabulary | 0.00 | 0.17 | 1.00 | **1.67** |

Three properties, none of them calibrated into the rule. In-distribution is **never** promoted, at any
depth — the error a storage policy must not make. New-combination converges to **exactly 1.00 =
ADDRESS and stops there**: it does not overshoot to the tier that costs 14× more, even as N keeps
growing and the fit term keeps scaling. New-vocabulary passes through ADDRESS and keeps climbing
toward EXTEND. The ladder is climbed in order and halted at the right rung, by an argmin over bits.

**The exchange rate, which is the actual answer to "how much does an OOD query warrant addition".**
Median coding gain and the batch size at which each tier pays for itself:

| population | ADDRESS gain | N* ADDRESS | EXTEND gain over ADDRESS | N* EXTEND |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| in-distribution | **−9.7 b/img** | 56 (7/10 never) | +21.1 | 388 |
| new-combination | +33.0 | **15** (1/10 never) | +87.6 | 93 |
| new-vocabulary | +22.6 | 18.5 (4/10 never) | **+218.0** | **36** |

So a single novel image does not justify even 72 bytes: 576 bits is ~10–20 % of one image's total
codelength and the measured gain is ~33 bits. **About fifteen instances justify an address, about
thirty-six justify a vocabulary extension.** That is a defensible answer, and it is one a threshold
cannot express.

**As a detector it is worse.** Separation of new-vocabulary from new-combination on N*: **AUROC 0.950**
against 6c's **0.995** on the plain novelty statistic. So this is not a better signal; it is a
different question asked of the same signal.

**What this establishes.** The tier a query warrants is **not a property of the query**. It is a
property of the query *and* how many examples of it have arrived, because the fit term in (*) scales
with N and the storage cost does not. A threshold is a function of the query alone, so 6c-bis's five
attempts were trying to answer an underdetermined question — a single explanation for five separate
failures, and a better one than any of the five individual causes. The practical consequence is that
the over-spend 6c-bis could not close is **not** a mis-placed threshold: at n18's operating point of
one query per concept, the correct MDL answer is to store nothing, and the system stores something
because the benchmark supplies one example where the accounting needs fifteen.

**Two bugs found here that would have manufactured a false positive**, recorded because both were
silent:

1. `elbo_bits` averages over the batch and already returns per-image bits; the diagnostic divided by
   `len(X)` a second time, making the fit term N·(code/N) = code and cancelling the batch-size
   scaling that is the entire claim. It would have surfaced as a **flat promotion curve** — which is
   indistinguishable from a genuine null.
2. `sbatch --export=ALL,SCRIPT="..."` **splits its value on commas**, so `--batch-sizes 1,2,4,8,16`
   arrived as `1` and the remainder became stray environment variables. Every run before 06:50
   measured N=1 only, and the job header printed `--batch-sizes 1` in plain sight while nothing
   failed. Pass colons under sbatch; `diag_mdl_router.py` now accepts both.

**Where this line stops.** The cascade stays as the shipped router — 6c's asymmetry argument is
unchanged and nothing measured here justifies moving it. `mdl_route` is kept for the diagnosis it
supports and for `mdl_crossover`, which turns "should I store this?" into "how many examples before
this is worth storing?", the form a lifelong stream can actually act on. The obvious next step is the
one this makes precise: **accumulate**. Hold novel queries in a buffer keyed by their inferred basis
row and promote when the accumulated gain crosses the tier cost, rather than deciding on arrival.
That is Cobweb's actual regime — it restructures as counts grow — and it is untested here.

## 5q2. The basis-vs-adapter comparison, run matched (and why the old one was not)

The claim in 5q -- that a basis direction beats a per-concept adapter -- took its two numbers from
two different runs. The basis figure (F1 0.755) came from `n11_basis_extension_nm`, which was invoked
with `--skip-adapter` and therefore contains no adapter arm at all; the adapter figure (0.658) came
from `n11_basis_extension_k4`, which is the run *without* norm-matching. Read within the run that
contains both, the ordering was the other way round: adapter 0.658 +/- 0.017 against basis 0.569 +/-
0.155.

So the comparison was re-run properly -- `--norm-match` with the adapter arm enabled, 3 seeds, both
arms scored on the same combinations of the same backbone.

| arm | joint | digit | fg | bg | F1 | recall | cost |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| basis, combinations it was **taught** on | 0.667 | 0.847 | 0.955 | 0.840 | 0.753 +/- 0.050 | 0.878 | 1024 bytes total |
| basis, combinations **never taught** | **0.676** | 0.868 | 0.938 | 0.840 | **0.756 +/- 0.032** | 0.860 | — |
| per-concept adapter | 0.532 | 0.651 | 0.828 | 0.944 | 0.662 +/- 0.081 | 0.823 | **551K per concept** |
| adapter on REAL images | 0.885 | 0.889 | 1.000 | 0.997 | 0.887 +/- 0.027 | 0.898 | 551K per concept |
| *real held-out images (ceiling)* | *0.910* | *0.910* | *1.000* | *1.000* | *0.913* | *0.912* | — |

**Three things this settles.**

1. **The basis does beat the adapter, at 2153x less cost** -- F1 0.756 against 0.662, from one
   256-float direction against 551K parameters per concept, and the direction is shared by every
   combination using that value while the adapter transfers to none of them.
2. **It is a basis, not a lookup.** Combinations the direction was never taught on score *the same*
   as the ones it was (0.676 against 0.667). That equality is the whole content of the word "basis";
   a memorised mapping would fall off.
3. **Norm-matching is load-bearing, not cosmetic.** The earlier contradictory result is exactly the
   failure 5q documents: left free, the optimizer returns a direction 15-20x larger than the basis it
   is joining, and F1 swings 0.65 / 0.35 / 0.71 across seeds. Constraining the new value to the scale
   of the factor's existing values removes the swing (sd 0.032). **The unstable variant is the one
   that lost to the adapter**, and reporting it against a norm-matched basis number from a different
   run was the error.

The methodological point is worth as much as the result: a claim assembled from two runs is not a
comparison, and this one happened to be wrong in the direction that flattered the method.


## 6d. Native parity: how close is a consolidated concept to one the backbone was TRAINED on? (N23)

The project's own sentence is "adapt new instances into the manifold in a way that is similar to if
they were learned with the existing ideas". Every result so far argues that indirectly -- preservation
is bit-exact, forgetting is 0.000, accuracy beats baselines -- because the comparison the sentence
actually names has never been available: *what that slot would have looked like had it never been
held out*.

`n1_train --all-slots` supplies it. A backbone trained on all 160 ColorMNIST slots (200 epochs,
conditional accuracy 0.922, unconditional validity 1.000) is not a method and is not competing; it is
the reference. 12 held-out compositions, 3 seeds.

| arm | joint | F1 | recall | content div |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| *real held-out images (data ceiling)* | *0.876 +/- 0.021* | *0.892 +/- 0.008* | *0.916 +/- 0.004* | *0.988* |
| **native** (oracle, trained on this slot) | **0.876 +/- 0.013** | **0.871 +/- 0.008** | **0.873 +/- 0.019** | 0.979 |
| **ours** (consolidated, never retrained) | 0.331 +/- 0.050 | 0.498 +/- 0.066 | 0.788 +/- 0.008 | 1.197 |
| **paper** (per-query LoRA from the PoE teacher) | 0.444 +/- 0.046 | 0.005 +/- 0.003 | 0.043 +/- 0.017 | 0.954 |

**The reference validates itself.** `native` sits at the data ceiling on joint accuracy (0.876 against
0.876), so a backbone that trained on a slot does reproduce it, and the denominator below is a real
upper bound rather than an artefact of a weak oracle.

**Parity, consolidated / native:**

| | joint | F1 | recall |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ours** | 0.379 +/- 0.060 | **0.571 +/- 0.073** | **0.903 +/- 0.024** |
| paper | 0.506 +/- 0.048 | 0.006 +/- 0.003 | 0.050 +/- 0.021 |

**The claim this supports, stated at the strength the numbers support it.** A concept consolidated
from a single out-of-distribution query, at **zero retraining and zero access to the held-out class**,
reaches **90% of native-level coverage** and **57% of native F1**. It does *not* reach native
per-sample fidelity: 38% on joint accuracy. So "as if it had been learned natively" is true of the
*extent* of the class the concept generates and false of the *precision* of each sample -- which is
the same split 5h certified from the other direction, where the conditioning span was shown able to
express one factor or the other but not both.

**And the contrast with the published consolidation is starker here than anywhere else.** Its per-query
LoRA scores *higher joint accuracy than ours* (0.444 against 0.331) and **0.6% of native F1**, because
recall 0.043 means it reproduces the query rather than the class. Measured against a backbone that
was trained on the slot, copying is visible in a way no within-method table shows it.

**Drag.** Seen-slot accuracy is 0.852 +/- 0.038 before consolidation and **0.852 +/- 0.038 after** --
identical, as it must be, since the base weights are frozen and a consolidated concept's adapter path
is inactive when sampling a base class. Read that as a consistency check on the invariant rather than
as evidence for it; the evidence is the bit-identical fingerprint test (6b). The oracle's 0.865 +/-
0.042 says retraining on all 160 slots cost it nothing on the 120 it already had, so the oracle is not
being flattered by the comparison either.


## 7a. CelebA, made trainable: factored conditioning (N19) — and two bugs found on the way

5o left CelebA measured and unusable: conditioning reached 34% of the real-data ceiling, and 2.3x the
data with 140 epochs moved it to 33%. That flatness is what made the diagnosis credible -- the
weakness was the conditioning DESIGN, not compute. A per-class embedding row over a 14-bit attribute
combination is estimated from as few as 25 images and shares nothing with the combination that
differs from it on one bit.

**The fix** (`unet.py cond_mode="factored"`): one embedding per (attribute, value), summed. Every
attribute vector is then estimated from every image carrying that value -- 478 (Bald) to 21663
(No_Beard) rather than 25 -- and training is no longer restricted to combinations with enough support
to own a row, so ~96k images are usable instead of ~5k. The conditioning head is **7168 parameters
against 308480** for a per-class table over the same data.

**Per-attribute margin over unconditional, which is what 5o measured:**

| backbone | margin |
|---|---|
| 5o: per-class embedding, 60 ep, 5k images | +0.048 |
| factored, corrected, 20 ep | +0.049 |
| factored, 60 ep, `Smiling x Big_Nose` withheld | +0.054 |
| factored, 60 ep, `Gray_Hair` withheld | +0.055 |
| **factored, 60 ep, combination holdout** | **+0.081** |

**1.7x the design 5o called "not ready to compose on"**, and matched at 20 epochs what per-class
needed 60 to reach.

### Two bugs, both found by looking at controls rather than at results

**The uint8 fast path trained in a different layout than it sampled.** This repository's flat vectors
are HWC as the loaders write them, and every consumer -- `_as_img`, the float branch of `train_ddpm`,
the sampler -- REINTERPRETS them as CHW. That is a fixed permutation, and because training and
sampling apply the same one the model simply learns in the permuted space while `rgb_img` displays
flat vectors as HWC, so images are shown correctly. The uint8 branch added to fit 96k images in RAM
transposed HWC -> NCHW instead, i.e. it "corrected" the layout: the backbone learned upright faces and
was then sampled through the CHW reinterpretation. Every CelebA number measured through the sampler
was meaningless -- margin +0.011, per-attribute controllability **0.000 on eight of eight attributes**,
and unconditional samples that were tiled mosaics rather than faces.

It was not the numbers that exposed it. They were merely bad, and "undertrained" was a plausible
story. It was the **sample grid**: controllability of exactly 0.000 on easy attributes like hair
colour is too clean to be undertraining. `tests/test_layout.py` now compares the two training
branches and the sampler's view on the same bytes -- one second on CPU, all deltas 0.000e+00 -- and
would have caught it before three GPU-hours. ColorMNIST never used that branch and is unaffected.

**The probe readability filter admitted blind probes.** `attr_accuracy` excluded attributes whose
probe scored under 0.65 plain accuracy. For a rare attribute that is the wrong criterion: Gray_Hair is
ON in 4.3% of CelebA, so a probe answering "no" every time scores 0.957. Measured on 8000 real images
(`tests/diag_probe_balance.py`), five of the twelve attributes the filter admitted cannot recognise
their own attribute --

| attribute | plain acc | TPR |
|---|---|---|
| Oval_Face | 0.712 | **0.066** |
| Brown_Hair | 0.778 | **0.171** |
| Bald | 0.977 | **0.206** |
| Gray_Hair | 0.962 | **0.321** |
| No_Beard | 0.883 | TNR **0.303** |

-- while Wavy_Hair, which the filter REJECTED at 0.533, is usable with TPR 0.908. Wrong in both
directions. `probe_usable` now requires TPR >= 0.5 and balanced accuracy >= 0.65.

**Both bugs were caught by the real-data ceiling row**, which is the reason this repository reports it
beside every method. A mismatched probe cache showed up as REAL held-out images scoring 0.557 where
the same images score 0.770; a blind probe showed up as a real-image ceiling of 0.25 on an attribute
whose probe claimed 0.96. In both cases every arm moved down together, which reads as "the method does
not work" rather than "this measurement is invalid" -- the most expensive confusion available, and one
a method-only table cannot detect at all.


## 7b. CelebA composition (N20): the separability threat, tested

Threat 7 has stood since the beginning: every composition result rested on ColorMNIST, whose digit /
foreground / background occupy DISJOINT pixels, and that disjointness is load-bearing -- a
per-dimension product of experts can assemble an unseen pairing because each pixel belongs to exactly
one factor. CelebA attributes overlap. This is the test.

Two splits, in increasing strength. Guidance is calibrated per backbone on TRAINED combinations, which
uses no held-out label (5p's lesson); it chose 4.0 and 6.0.

### The strong split: `Smiling x Big_Nose` never co-occur

Every combination in which both are ON is withheld -- 14620 images across 111 combinations -- while
49874 and 25162 images still carry each attribute ALONE. Only the CO-OCCURRENCE is novel, which is the
direct analogue of ColorMNIST's held-out `(fg, bg)` pairs, and the two attributes are adjacent facial
regions that jointly reshape the lower face, so the split is not separable.

`pair` = both attributes correct AT ONCE. 12 held-out combinations, 24 samples each, **3 seeds**.

| method | **pair** | target | joint | mean-attr | precision | recall | F1 | content div |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *real held-out images* | *0.801 ± 0.137* | *0.656* | *0.343* | *0.827* | *1.000* | *0.884* | *0.937* | *1.007* |
| **ours** (composed vector, 0 params) | **0.903 ± 0.094** | **0.727** | **0.367** | **0.850** | 0.667 | 0.579 | 0.598 | 1.068 |
| `query-only` | 0.777 ± 0.117 | 0.620 | 0.263 | 0.778 | 0.804 | **0.018** | **0.031** | 0.295 |
| `poe-guided` (the published method) | 0.463 ± 0.140 | 0.436 | 0.105 | 0.593 | 0.145 | **0.000** | **0.000** | 0.432 |
| `nearest-class` (retrieval control) | **0.355 ± 0.186** | 0.235 | 0.082 | 0.628 | 0.477 | 0.630 | 0.505 | 1.212 |

**Seed spread on `pair` is large (±0.09–0.19) and single-seed numbers mislead here** — seed 0 alone
reads `ours` 0.72 and `nearest-class` 0.10, against 3-seed means of 0.903 and 0.355. The orderings
hold on every seed; the magnitudes do not, which is why only the pooled table is quoted.

### The weaker split: 24 whole combinations withheld

| method | target | joint | mean-attr | precision | recall | F1 | content div |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *real held-out images* | *0.38* | *0.44* | *0.89* | *1.00* | *0.86* | *0.92* | *0.98* |
| **ours** | **0.41** | **0.59** | **0.92** | 0.65 | 0.64 | **0.64** | 1.12 |
| `query-only` | 0.34 | 0.33 | 0.84 | 0.66 | 0.01 | 0.01 | 0.36 |
| `poe-guided` | 0.30 | 0.07 | 0.70 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.38 |
| `nearest-class` | 0.15 | 0.26 | 0.81 | 0.48 | 0.53 | 0.48 | 1.23 |

### What this settles, and what it does not

**Composing known factor values produces a combination that was never observed, when the factors
overlap the same pixels.** On the strong split the composed vector reaches `pair` **0.903 +/- 0.094**
where retrieval from the nearest trained combination reaches **0.355 +/- 0.186** -- and retrieval
falling short is not a weak baseline but a structural fact, since every trained neighbour differs on
at least one of the two withheld attributes. Threat 7 is addressed rather than deferred.

**Scoring above the real-image ceiling is expected here.** `ours` at 0.903 against real images at
0.801 repeats what E0 recorded on ColorMNIST: a generated sample is a cleaner, more prototypical rendering
than a photograph, and a linear probe reads it more easily. It means the probe ceiling is not a hard
bound on generated data, not that composition beats reality; the informative comparison is against the
baselines, all of which are scored the same way.

**Both published baselines fail in the documented way.** `poe-guided` reaches recall 0.00 and F1 0.00
on both splits at content diversity 0.38-0.44 -- it emits near-copies. `query-only` is the sharper
demonstration: on the strong split it scores `pair` 0.777, close to the real-image ceiling, at recall
**0.018 +/- 0.009** and diversity **0.295**. It is reproducing the query, which happens to carry the right
attributes because it is an image of the target combination. Attribute accuracy alone cannot separate
composing from copying; that is why coverage is reported beside it everywhere in this document.

**Stated as a limitation:** the guidance calibration selected the TOP of its grid (6.0) on the strong
split, so the operating point sits at a boundary and may not be optimal -- the sweep should be
extended before these numbers are treated as the method's best. The grid was widened from 2.0 to 6.0
on the evidence of `diag_celeba_guidance.py`; capping it at 2.0, as the first version did, would have
understated the result substantially.


## 7c. CelebA vocabulary (N21): the routing rule's second half, on overlapping attributes

5q's rule has two halves and CelebA tests both. 7b showed the first -- a new COMBINATION of known
values is expressible. This is the second: an attribute whose ON value the backbone has **never
seen** (`--holdout-attr Gray_Hair`, 4452 images withheld entirely), 4 combinations teach the vector
and 8 more are never taught, 3 seeds.

| arm | the withheld attribute | % of ceiling | cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| *real held-out images (ceiling)* | *0.420 ± 0.163* | — | — |
| **adapter trained on REAL images** | **0.361 ± 0.153** | **86%** | 726K per concept |
| learned conditioning vector | 0.141 ± 0.112 | 34% | 1024 bytes |
| untrained vector (floor) | 0.118 ± 0.100 | 28% | 0 |

**The learned and untrained conditioning vectors are not separated** (0.141 ± 0.112 against 0.118 ±
0.100). On ColorMNIST the same arm scored exactly 0.000; here it is indistinguishable from noise.
Either way the conclusion is the one 5q reached and it now holds on a second, harder benchmark:
**conditioning cannot fabricate a factor value the backbone never generated, and only a weight update
from real data recovers it.**

Absolute values are compressed because this attribute's probe has TPR 0.32 -- its own ceiling is only
0.420 -- which is exactly why the ceiling row is reported beside every arm, and why the honest
statement is "86% of what real images achieve" rather than an absolute.

**Preservation is structural here too: 171/171** compositions not using the extended attribute are
bit-identical after the extension. Nothing they read was written.

## 7d. CelebA retention (N22): is preservation still structural when factors overlap?

N5's result is the one the paper rests on, and it was measured only on ColorMNIST: arms that
structurally *remove* shared parameters forget exactly 0.000, arms that try to *protect* them forget.
Preservation here is claimed to be **structural** -- a frozen ancestor path plus a private
conditioning vector cannot be written by a later concept -- and a structural argument should be
indifferent to whether the factors overlap in pixel space. So this is a test the method can fail.

12 held-out combinations of REAL images, arriving in batches of 3.

| arm | mean-attribute ACC | forgetting | **fraction bit-identical** | adapter params |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ours** | 0.848 | **0.000** | **1.00** | 12.35M |
| **independent** (parameter isolation) | 0.830 | **0.000** | **1.00** | 9.44M |
| finetune | 0.862 | 0.012 | 0.25 | 9.44M |
| ewc | 0.864 | 0.009 | 0.25 | 9.44M |
| replay | 0.817 | 0.043 | 0.25 | 9.44M |

**The `exact` column is the result, and it separates perfectly**: 1.00 for both arms that remove
shared parameters, 0.25 for all three that try to protect them. Accuracy does not separate the arms
at all here (0.817–0.864, with `ewc` nominally highest) — which is the point worth taking from this
table. On a metric as insensitive as a 12-attribute mean, a method can look fine while having
overwritten three quarters of what it previously held. Forgetting measured as accuracy drift would
have reported 0.009 for `ewc` and called it nearly perfect; forgetting measured as *did the earlier
concept survive unchanged* reports that three of four did not.

`tgtACC`/`tgtFGT` are NaN throughout: the stricter probe filter (7a) excludes the differing attribute
for most combination-split cases, so that column has nothing to score. Mean-attribute ACC and the
bit-identical fraction are the usable metrics and are what is quoted.


## 5l-bis. The published method was being run in OUR configuration, not its own (corrected)

The case-study figure showed `poe-guided` emitting the query plus speckle. That was our fault, and the
correction changes every number in the comparison — in the baseline's favour, and in favour of the
argument.

**The mechanism.** Their Eq. 12 gives the teacher's epsilon a precision factor
`(abar*Sigma_T + (1-abar) I)^-1`, which blows up as `abar -> 1`. Their Eq. 14 exists to cancel exactly
that: `w(t) = min(w0 * tr(Sigma_t)/d, wmax)` shrinks guidance as the teacher concentrates. The
`poe-guided` arm inherited this repository's `schedule="anneal"`, which does the opposite — it rises
to `wmax` as `u -> 0`. And the justification for annealing, recorded in `cfg_weight`'s own docstring,
is that the ANALYTIC Gaussian mixture has no density at an unseen pairing; a convolutional UNet shares
weights across colours and has no such hole, so the rationale never transferred to the backbone the
baseline actually runs on.

**Two checks before accepting the diagnosis**, because a better-looking row is not an explanation:

* `tests/diag_poe_faithful.py` sweeps the sampler. With their Eq. 14 and the raw Laplace covariance
  the SAME teacher produces clean, correct digits at accuracy **0.865** against our default's 0.521.
* `tests/diag_poe_modes.py` rules out a degenerate teacher: the selected expert means are spread
  **0.229**, WIDER than the real class spread of 0.148, at `sigma_T` 0.082. Widening the discovery
  grid to higher noise moves accuracy (0.521 -> 0.635) and leaves recall at 0.010. So the coverage
  failure is not a discovery artefact either; it is the contraction 5e already identified — CFG
  toward a unimodal Gaussian pulls every trajectory to its mean.

**Corrected comparison, 3 seeds, baseline in its own configuration:**

| ColorMNIST | joint | precision | recall | F1 | content div |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *real held-out images* | *0.864* | *1.000* | *0.939* | *0.969* | *0.991* |
| ours | 0.314 | 0.400 | **0.810** | **0.498** | 1.209 |
| `poe-guided` (their Eq. 14) | **0.817** | **0.839** | **0.001** | 0.003 | 0.269 |
| `nearest-class` | 0.000 | 0.008 | 0.066 | 0.005 | 1.050 |

| CelebA, strong split | pair | precision | recall | F1 | content div |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *real held-out images* | *0.801* | *1.000* | *0.884* | *0.937* | *1.007* |
| ours | **0.903** | 0.667 | **0.579** | **0.598** | 1.068 |
| `poe-guided` (their Eq. 14) | 0.645 | **0.944** | **0.000** | 0.000 | 0.147 |

**What changed, and why it matters.** Under our schedule the baseline had precision 0.039 and looked
broken. Under its own it has precision **0.839** (ColorMNIST) and **0.944** (CelebA): its samples are
genuinely plausible members of the target class, and on ColorMNIST it beats us on accuracy *and*
precision. What it does not have is coverage — recall 0.001 and 0.000, content diversity 0.27 and
0.15 against a real class spread of ~1.0.

So the claim is not "the published method fails". It is: **the published method produces excellent
samples of one point in the class, and per-sample metrics cannot tell that apart from producing the
class.** That is a stronger statement than the one this document made before, it is made with the
baseline at its best rather than at our worst, and it is the statement the coverage metric exists to
support.

**Scope, unchanged from 6.** This is our reimplementation on our backbone under our metrics. Wang et
al. report recall 80.7% on their own setup with Inception-feature precision/recall and a ~35M model
on 120k images; nothing here contradicts that, and nothing here is a reproduction of their tables.
What is now fair is the *internal* comparison: every arm in these tables is sampled from the same
backbone, scored by the same probes, and configured the way its authors specify.


---

## 9. Baselines added after a systematic literature review — and three defects it exposed in ours

Two structured reviews of the composition and continual-learning literatures were run against this
repository. The useful output was not the list of new arms; it was a list of ways we were running
*existing* baselines weakly. Those come first, because they are the same failure mode as §5l-bis.

### 9a. Three defects in our own code

**(1) `consolidate()` raised `NameError` on every single-concept call.** The regularizer hook
(`begin_task`) was added at both optimizer sites, but the single-concept `consolidate()` never
declared the `ewc` parameter it referenced. Only `consolidate_batch()` was exercised by the CL
experiments, so nothing caught it. Fixed, and the penalty plus the SI step hooks are now wired
through that path too.

**(2) Composable Diffusion was run at `w_i = 1/n`.** Liu et al.'s `w_i` are per-concept *guidance
scales* meant to add — the sum of residuals is what makes the operator a product rather than a mean
— and they run values around 5–7.5. Dividing by `n` pins total guidance at 1.0 regardless of how
many concepts are composed, which is approximately their **average**, and gets weaker as more
concepts are added. This is the identical error to §5l-bis, where running the published method on
our schedule instead of its own Eq. 14 took its precision from 0.039 to 0.839. The weight is now
swept, not defaulted (§9b).

**(3) The textual-inversion baseline was confined to `span(C)`.** `learn_embedding` optimizes
`alpha` over trained class embeddings. That restriction is a *claim our method makes* — that an
unseen composition is expressible in the model's existing vocabulary — so imposing it on the
baseline assumes exactly what is under test, and handicaps it precisely when the held-out
combination falls outside the hull of seen classes. Textual Inversion as published (Gal et al.)
optimizes a **free vector in R^d**. Added as `free=True` and reported as a separate
`inversion-free` arm; the span-restricted version stays as ours.

### 9b. Composable Diffusion, swept rather than defaulted

`tests/diag_composable_scale.py`, ColorMNIST, 3 held-out compositions, top-3 slots, 16 samples:

| scale | joint | digit | fg | bg | precision | recall |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.33 | **0.000** | 0.062 | 0.62 | 0.27 | 0.000 | 0.630 |
| 1.00 | **0.000** | 0.000 | 0.48 | 0.23 | 0.000 | 0.120 |
| 2.00 | **0.000** | 0.146 | 0.10 | 0.42 | 0.000 | 0.367 |
| 3.00 | **0.000** | 0.167 | 0.04 | 0.27 | 0.000 | 0.007 |
| 5.00 | **0.000** | 0.104 | 0.04 | 0.31 | 0.000 | 0.000 |
| 7.50 | **0.000** | 0.000 | 0.10 | 0.33 | 0.000 | 0.000 |

Joint accuracy is **0.000 at every scale**, and precision is 0.000 at every scale. What the scale
buys is the usual guidance-versus-diversity trade in the *wrong* place: at 0.33 the arm has recall
0.630 with precision 0.000, i.e. it is diverse and wrong.

This is the operator's own precondition failing, not a tuning failure, and it is worth stating
plainly because it bounds what score addition can do here. Composable Diffusion composes *trained
conditionals*. ColorMNIST's backbone has no conditional for "cyan foreground" and none for
"deep-red background" — it has 120 entangled slot conditionals. Adding their scores cannot isolate a
factor that no conditional represents. Having swept it across a 23× range, the row can no longer be
read as a strawman.

**CelebA is the case where the method genuinely applies**, because the factored head provides a real
per-attribute conditional. There it composes exactly the conditionals our method composes, in score
space rather than embedding space — the sharpest available comparison of the two operators — and it
is reported at the scale chosen by its own sweep.

### 9c. The strongest objection to our own operator, now an arm

Du et al. (ICML 2023) prove that for a product `q^1 · q^2`,

```
grad log q~_t^prod(x_t)  !=  grad log q_t^1(x_t) + grad log q_t^2(x_t)
```

The sum of diffused scores is the score of the **product of diffused marginals**, not the
**diffusion of the product**. Every method that pushes a composed score through DDIM or ancestral
sampling — Composable Diffusion, the PoE teacher, and *ours* — is therefore targeting the wrong
distribution, and the blame is the **sampler's**, not the model's. Their fix is extra MCMC
transitions per noise level, which takes CLEVR 5-cube composition from 70.8% to 91.6% and ImageNet
classifier guidance from 18.6% to 94.6%, at ~5× sampling cost.

It is training-free and exemplar-free, so it is unambiguously fair here. Implemented as annealed
unadjusted Langevin (`score.py`, `mcmc=dict(steps, lam)`) — MALA's accept/reject would need an
energy parameterization a pretrained eps-net does not provide — and reported as `composable-mcmc`.

### 9d. Continual-learning arms added

The retention tables previously compared only against CL methods derived from the *classifier*
literature. Added, all on the same backbone and optimizer budget:

- **SI** (Zenke et al. 2017) — path-integral importance accumulated online. The cleanest transfer in
  its family: it inspects only gradients and weight trajectories, never output semantics, so it
  applies to an eps-regression objective without reinterpretation.
- **MAS** (Aljundi et al. 2018) — importance as the sensitivity of the squared output norm. Derived
  for an *arbitrary* vector output with no labels and no softmax, so setting `F = eps_theta` is not
  an adaptation of the method, it *is* the method.
- **DER++** (Buzzega et al. 2020) — the strongest simple baseline in modern CL. Its alpha term
  matches a prediction recorded mid-trajectory; its beta term is plain exemplar replay.
- **generative distillation** (Masip et al., CoLLAs 2025) — see below.

**Why `gen-distill` had to be added rather than just reporting `replay`.** Naive generative replay
is a *documented pathology* for diffusion: Masip et al. measure it at **FID 211.8 against plain
finetuning's 68.8** — nearly 3× worse than no protection at all — because self-generated samples are
regressed onto the noise drawn for them, so their errors are treated as ground truth and compound.
Distillation instead matches the **pre-task model's own eps-prediction** on the same `(x_t, t)`,
transferring the whole reverse process, and recovers FID 23.5 against a joint bound of 15.5.
Reporting `replay` alone would be reporting a known-broken configuration as a baseline.

### 9e. DER++ at ACC 0.250 was our bug, not a result

The first DER++ run came in at **ACC 0.250, BWT −0.263** — far below plain finetuning's 0.750. That
reads as a damning result for the strongest baseline in the CL literature, which is exactly why it
was worth not believing.

The cause: DER's stored target `z = h(x)` is a function of a **fixed** input. Here the network's
input is `x_t = sqrt(abar) x0 + sqrt(1-abar) e`, and the buffer was redrawing `e` at replay time.
The stored eps-prediction therefore described a *different image* than the one being presented — an
incoherent target, and a strong one, applied at every step. Storing the noise draw alongside the
sample fixes it:

| DER++ | ACC | BWT | FGT |
|---|---|---|---|
| redrawn noise (wrong) | 0.250 | −0.263 | 0.263 |
| stored noise (correct) | **0.781** | −0.025 | 0.025 |

Third measurement bug in this project found by a control row rather than by a result.

### 9f. Two columns the tables were missing

**Generation quality.** Probe accuracy is *fidelity-blind*: an arm can keep the attributes legible
while its images degrade, and the column will not show it. Masip et al.'s numbers are the proof —
generative replay scores *higher* classifier accuracy than finetuning (0.50 vs 0.17) at 3× worse
FID, so an accuracy-only table ranks the broken arm first. Both retention tables now track a
per-concept Frechet distance on the same schedule as the accuracy matrix and report CLoG's **AFQ**
(final quality) and **FR** (its degradation).

**What each arm must keep.** A `stored` column counts the real images an arm retains forever: 0 for
ours and for every regularizer, the buffer size for DER++, and the entire stream for TTT. This is
the axis on which rehearsal methods lose to an insert-only bank, and it was invisible in a table of
accuracies.

**Reference-free diversity and a copying check.** Every other metric here is anchored to real
images, and for a held-out combination that anchoring is weakest exactly where our claim is
strongest — a *correct* novel sample is absent from the training manifold by construction, so
precision and density measured against it are anti-correlated with the property under test. The
**Vendi score** (Friedman & Dieng, TMLR 2023) needs no reference at all. **`memorization()`** is
MOSAIC's `d_NN1/d_NN2 < 1/3` test against the images the backbone actually trained on, because a
method that scores well by *retrieving* a training image is indistinguishable from one that composes
in every aggregate metric above.

### 9g. The uncomfortable one: a corrected baseline nearly catches us on CelebA

Fixing the `1/n` handicap did not just make Composable Diffusion look better — it made it a genuinely
competitive method, and on CelebA it very nearly matches ours. This is recorded in full rather than
summarised, because it changes what the paper is entitled to claim.

**Strong split** (`Smiling × Big_Nose` never co-occur in training), seed 0, 3 cases, 16 samples.
`pair` = both withheld attributes correct at once, the metric the split exists to measure:

| arm | scale | pair | target | joint | mean_attr | precision | recall | content div |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| real | — | 0.48 | 0.65 | 0.19 | 0.77 | 1.00 | 0.95 | 1.01 |
| **ours** | — | **0.71** | 0.73 | 0.25 | 0.84 | 0.71 | **0.60** | **1.06** |
| composable-diff | 1.0 | 0.38 | 0.65 | 0.00 | 0.67 | 0.38 | 0.61 | 1.25 |
| composable-diff | 3.0 | 0.69 | **0.75** | 0.17 | 0.80 | 0.60 | 0.62 | 1.12 |
| composable-diff | 5.0 | 0.71 | **0.75** | 0.21 | 0.83 | 0.79 | 0.51 | 0.96 |
| composable-diff | 7.5 | **0.73** | 0.73 | 0.29 | 0.86 | **0.92** | 0.30 | 0.77 |
| nearest-class | — | 0.12 | 0.17 | 0.00 | 0.53 | 0.38 | 0.61 | 1.27 |

At `w = 1/n` — the configuration we had — it scores `pair` 0.38 and looks comfortably beaten. At its
own best scale it scores **0.73 against our 0.71**. A 2022 method with **zero parameters and no
training** matches us on the metric this benchmark was built to test.

**What the extra columns show, and why they are the answer rather than an excuse.** Read across the
scale sweep: as `w` rises, precision climbs 0.38 → 0.92 while recall falls 0.61 → 0.30 and content
diversity falls 1.25 → 0.77, passing *through* the real data's 1.01 and out the other side. That is
the same accuracy-for-coverage trade `poe-guided` makes in §5e, just at a gentler slope — strong
guidance contracts trajectories, and the pool stops covering the composition. At its most accurate
setting the baseline emits samples that are 92% correct and half as varied as real data.

Ours sits at `pair` 0.71 with recall 0.60 and diversity 1.06 — matching real data's spread. The
defensible claim is therefore **not** that our composition operator is better on CelebA. It is that
it reaches equivalent composition accuracy at roughly **twice the coverage**, and that is only
visible because we report recall and diversity next to accuracy.

> **Superseded by §11c.** This section is a single seed, and against `composable-diff` only. At
> three seeds, with Du et al.'s MCMC-corrected version of the same operator in the table, even the
> "equivalent accuracy" half does not survive: `composable-mcmc` is *more* accurate than ours and
> does not pay the coverage price. §11c is the number to quote; this section is kept for the
> reasoning, which is unchanged.

**The honest bottom line for the composition claim, which is now benchmark-dependent:**

- **CelebA** — our operator does **not** beat score addition. Where the model has genuine
  per-attribute conditionals, adding their scores works about as well as composing in embedding
  space. We hold a coverage advantage at equal accuracy, and that is the whole of it.
- **ColorMNIST** — our operator **strictly dominates**: composable-diff is at 0.000 joint accuracy at
  every scale across a 23× range (§9b), because there is no per-factor conditional to add. Ours is
  not, because it composes in a factor basis recovered from the entangled embeddings rather than in
  score space.

That contrast is a *result*, not a hedge: it identifies the precondition under which the cheaper
operator suffices, and score addition is strictly cheaper when it does. What it costs us is the
claim to a better composition operator in general.

**None of this touches the lifelong claims.** Composable Diffusion produces a sample and keeps
nothing — no concept is registered, nothing is preserved, nothing accumulates, and the next query
starts from the frozen model again. Retention (§7d, N22), bit-exact preservation, the routing
decision (§6c, N18), growth accounting (§5z, N24) and native parity (§6d, N23) are all measured
against arms that *do* retain, and are unaffected. The paper's spine is the lifelong mechanism; the
composition operator is inherited, and on one of two benchmarks it is not improved.

## 10. The metric audit: what each arm is allowed to be measured on

Prompted by a simple question — are we reporting the axes this method actually wins on, and are we
reporting the ones it loses on? Two columns were missing in each direction.

### 10a. Missing columns that favour us

| metric | what it asks | why the old table could not show it |
|---|---|---|
| **A** (Díaz-Rodríguez et al. 2018) | mean accuracy over the WHOLE lower triangle | final-row `ACC` cannot distinguish *never dropped* from *dropped and recovered by the end* |
| **REM** = 1 − \|min(BWT,0)\| | remembering, normalized to [0,1] | same information as BWT, in the direction where 1.0 is the good end |
| **SSS** = 1 − Mem(buffer)/Mem(stream) | what the arm must KEEP | invisible in a table of accuracies; ours is exactly **1.0**, DER++ and TTT are not |
| **stored images** | the same thing, unnormalized | — |

`SSS` is the sharpest of these. The n5 table shows `der++` at ACC 0.792 and `ttt` at 0.797 —
statistically indistinguishable from our 0.792 — but `der++` holds a permanent 200-image buffer and
`ttt` keeps all **768** images of the stream forever, while ours keeps none. An accuracy-only table
reports those three arms as equivalent. They are not.

### 10b. A missing column that does NOT favour us

**MS** (model-size efficiency) is reported for the same reason. An insert-only bank grows, so MS is
the one axis where the honest number is against us, and the n5 table makes it concrete: `independent`
reaches **ACC 0.839** with **7.16M** adapter parameters where ours reaches **0.792** with **8.82M**.
Parameter isolation without a hierarchy is *better on both axes here*.

That is not a new finding — §5s already established the hierarchy does not earn its keep on this
dataset — but it does bound what n5 can be used to claim. **n5 does not show our method beating
parameter isolation; it shows both of them beating everything that tries to protect shared weights.**
The differentiator against `independent` is not in this table at all — it is the routing rule (§6c)
and the growth accounting (§5z), where an *arrangement* costs a 1024-byte basis direction instead of
a 551K-parameter adapter. A reader who takes n5 alone would be entitled to prefer `independent`, and
the paper should say so rather than let the ACC column imply otherwise.

### 10c. What the new quality columns did and did not separate

`AFQ`/`FR` (CLoG's generative-quality protocol on a pixel-PCA Fréchet distance) came out at
0.030–0.040 for **every** arm with `FR` at +0.000 throughout. So on this benchmark the arms are not
distinguishable by sample fidelity — the differences are in *which* attributes get generated, not in
image quality. That is a null result, it is reported as one, and it means the accuracy columns are
not hiding a fidelity collapse the way Masip et al. found for generative replay on Fashion-MNIST.

The one arm where it *should* have bitten is `replay`, which collapses to **ACC 0.479 / BWT −0.307**
— and its AFQ is 0.032, in line with everything else. Its failure is compositional, not visual.

### 10d. Where each claim is actually settled

Written down because the audit's main output was realising that three different tables were being
asked to support the same sentence:

| claim | the table that settles it | the table that does NOT |
|---|---|---|
| concepts are preserved exactly | n5/n22 `retention_exact` = 1.00 vs 0.25 | `ACC`, where `mas` also reaches FGT 0.000 |
| retention costs no stored data | n5/n22 `SSS` = 1.0 vs `der++`, `ttt` | `ACC`, which ranks them equal |
| cost scales with vocabulary, not experience | n24 growth accounting | n5, where we are *worse* than `independent` |
| a consolidated concept is usable | n23 native parity | n9 composition accuracy alone |
| composition beats score addition | n9 (ColorMNIST) only | n20 (CelebA), where it does not — §9g |

## 11. The full baseline set, run (seed 1 shown; 3 seeds in TABLES.md)

### 11a. ColorMNIST — every training-free composition baseline, at its own best setting

| method | joint | digit | fg | bg | precision | recall | F1 | content div |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *real* | *0.85* | *0.85* | *1.00* | *1.00* | *1.00* | *0.96* | *0.98* | *1.01* |
| **ours** | 0.28 | 0.50 | 0.75 | 0.82 | 0.47 | **0.79** | **0.56** | 1.17 |
| poe-guided (their Eq. 14) | **0.74** | 1.00 | 0.96 | 0.78 | 0.75 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.32 |
| composable-diff | 0.00 | 0.17 | 0.41 | 0.27 | 0.03 | 0.11 | 0.03 | 1.26 |
| composable-mcmc | 0.00 | 0.24 | 0.46 | 0.20 | 0.01 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.51 |
| inversion (span-restricted) | 0.23 | 0.37 | 0.58 | 0.77 | 0.30 | 0.67 | 0.37 | 1.06 |
| inversion-free (Gal et al. as published) | 0.03 | 0.28 | 0.62 | 0.42 | 0.04 | 0.79 | 0.08 | 1.28 |
| nearest-class | 0.00 | 0.17 | 0.80 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.00 | 0.97 |

Three things worth stating.

**Score addition fails here regardless of sampler.** `composable-diff` is 0.00 and Du et al.'s MCMC
correction (`composable-mcmc`) does not rescue it — 0.00 as well, with diversity *falling* 1.26 →
0.51. That is the expected result and it sharpens their claim rather than contradicting it: a better
sampler corrects the distribution a composed score *targets*, and here the composed score is built
from entangled slot conditionals that contain no per-factor information to begin with. The sampler
was never the binding constraint on this benchmark.

**The free-vector inversion baseline is much worse than the span-restricted one** (F1 0.08 vs 0.37),
which is evidence *for* our parameterization rather than against it. Optimizing a free vector in R^d
from a single query has nothing holding it in the region of conditioning space the model decodes
well; its recall is high (0.79) and its precision is 0.04, i.e. it produces variety that is not the
target. This is the arm that was previously missing, and it happens to support the design.

**`poe-guided` still wins on accuracy and still scores F1 0.00.** Unchanged from §5l-bis, and it is
the whole reason F1 rather than accuracy is the headline: 0.74 joint accuracy at recall 0.000.

### 11b. CelebA, strong split — where a 2022 baseline catches us

`Smiling` × `Big_Nose` never co-occur in training. `pair` = both correct at once.

| method | pair | target | joint | mean_attr | precision | recall | content div |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *real* | *0.88* | *0.59* | *0.36* | *0.83* | *1.00* | *0.89* | *1.01* |
| **ours** | **1.00** | 0.74 | 0.36 | 0.86 | 0.68 | **0.52** | **1.04** |
| composable-diff | **1.00** | 0.76 | 0.37 | 0.86 | 0.87 | 0.25 | 0.75 |
| composable-mcmc | 0.98 | **0.77** | 0.41 | 0.88 | 0.38 | 0.44 | 0.82 |
| poe-guided | 0.68 | 0.32 | 0.04 | 0.70 | 0.92 | 0.00 | 0.15 |
| query-only | 0.73 | 0.41 | 0.22 | 0.74 | 0.83 | 0.01 | 0.28 |
| nearest-class | 0.25 | 0.11 | 0.05 | 0.62 | 0.48 | 0.59 | 1.21 |

Ours and `composable-diff` **tie at `pair` 1.00**, and it edges us on `target` (0.76 vs 0.74). The
separation is entirely in coverage: **recall 0.52 vs 0.25** and **content diversity 1.04 vs 0.75**,
where real data sits at 1.01. We match the real spread; it produces half the variety.

Du et al.'s corrector behaves exactly as advertised *here*, where the conditionals are real: it moves
`composable-diff`'s recall 0.25 → 0.44 and its precision 0.87 → 0.38, i.e. it trades sharpness for
coverage and lands between the two. That is the first place in this repository where the MCMC
correction does something, and it is the place where the operator's precondition is actually met.

**The conclusion this table supports** is narrow and worth stating in exactly this form: on a
benchmark with genuine per-attribute conditionals, our composition operator is **not more accurate**
than adding scores — it reaches the same accuracy while covering twice as much of the class. On a
benchmark without them (§11a) score addition does not work at all. Neither statement is about
lifelong learning, which is measured elsewhere and where neither of these baselines competes: both
produce a sample and keep nothing.

### 11c. Three seeds, and the composition conclusion gets weaker again

Seed 1 alone (§11b) suggested a tie on accuracy with a coverage win for us. Across three seeds the
picture is worse than that, and the arm responsible is the one added *because* it was the strongest
published objection to our own operator.

**CelebA, strong split** (`Smiling` × `Big_Nose` never co-occur), mean ± s.e. over 3 seeds:

| method | pair | target | precision | recall | Vendi | content div |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *real* | *0.801±0.137* | *0.656±0.068* | *1.000* | *0.884* | *2.454* | *1.007* |
| **ours** | 0.903±0.094 | 0.727±0.081 | 0.667 | **0.579** | 2.250 | **1.068** |
| composable-diff | 0.934±0.066 | 0.740±0.084 | **0.856** | 0.280 | 1.736 | 0.750 |
| **composable-mcmc** | **0.965±0.023** | **0.779±0.105** | 0.390 | 0.483 | **2.285** | 0.838 |
| poe-guided | 0.645±0.189 | 0.488±0.087 | 0.944 | 0.000 | 1.048 | 0.147 |
| query-only | 0.777±0.117 | 0.620±0.132 | 0.804 | 0.018 | 1.180 | 0.295 |
| nearest-class | 0.355±0.186 | 0.235±0.071 | 0.477 | 0.630 | 2.512 | 1.212 |

**Du et al.'s MCMC-corrected score addition beats us on both accuracy metrics** — `pair` 0.965 vs
0.903, `target` 0.779 vs 0.727 — and it does *not* pay the coverage price `composable-diff` pays:
its Vendi is **2.285 against our 2.250**, i.e. slightly better, with recall 0.483 against our 0.579.

So the §9g framing ("equivalent accuracy at twice the coverage") survives only against
`composable-diff`. Against `composable-mcmc` our remaining advantages are **recall (0.579 vs 0.483)**
and **content diversity (1.068 vs 0.838, real 1.007)** — we match the real spread and it does not —
while it is more accurate. That is a narrower claim than the one this repository was making a day
ago, and it is the correct one.

On the **weaker CelebA split** the same arm is even more dominant on accuracy (`target` 0.718±0.063
vs our 0.511±0.054) and much weaker on coverage (recall 0.198 vs our 0.622), which is the familiar
sharpness-for-coverage trade at a more extreme setting.

**What survives, stated exactly.**

1. **On ColorMNIST our operator is the only one that works at all** — both composable arms are at
   0.000 across a 23× guidance sweep and with the MCMC corrector, because that backbone has no
   per-factor conditional to add (§11a). This is a real and reproducible separation.
2. **On CelebA our operator is not the best available.** A training-free 2023 sampler correction
   applied to a 2022 operator is more accurate. We keep an advantage in coverage, which matters for
   the copying argument, but we should not claim a better composition operator.
3. **None of this touches the lifelong results**, and this is not a rhetorical escape: `composable-diff`
   and `composable-mcmc` produce a sample and register nothing. They cannot be asked what they
   retain, what they cost on the hundredth concept, or whether an earlier concept survived — which is
   what §7d, §5z and §6d measure, and where the contribution is.

The composition stage should therefore be presented as **inherited and characterized**, not as
improved: the useful contribution there is knowing *when* score addition suffices (CelebA: it does,
and better) and when it cannot work at all (ColorMNIST: no per-factor conditional exists).

### 11d. ColorMNIST, re-run with the guidance calibrated (3 seeds)

The pool-guidance weight had been left at 1.0 on this benchmark while CelebA calibrated its own
(§10 / `tests/diag_ours_quality.py`).  Calibrating it label-free on TRAINED slots selects **1.5**
(trained-slot joint 0.969 against 0.781 at 1.0, and 0.062 at 3.0 where the colour factor breaks).
Re-running the whole showdown with it:

| method | joint | digit | fg | bg | precision | recall | F1 | Vendi | content div | memorized |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *real* | *0.864±0.023* | *0.864* | *1.000* | *0.997* | *1.000* | *0.939* | *0.969* | *1.901* | *0.991* | *0.000* |
| **ours** | 0.539±0.025 | 0.739 | 0.828 | 0.911 | 0.561 | 0.778 | **0.605±0.043** | **1.942** | **1.112** | 0.000 |
| poe-guided | **0.839±0.049** | 0.997 | 0.983 | 0.856 | 0.839 | 0.001 | 0.003±0.003 | 1.075 | 0.269 | 0.000 |
| composable-diff | 0.000 | 0.144 | 0.458 | 0.192 | 0.022 | 0.134 | 0.030 | 2.222 | 1.223 | 0.000 |
| composable-mcmc | 0.003 | 0.264 | 0.667 | 0.122 | 0.056 | 0.134 | 0.000 | 1.617 | 0.678 | 0.000 |
| inversion (span) | 0.403±0.035 | 0.656 | 0.858 | 0.728 | 0.489 | 0.799 | 0.545 | 2.015 | 1.075 | 0.000 |
| inversion-free | 0.169±0.080 | 0.461 | 0.628 | 0.672 | 0.242 | 0.845 | 0.332 | 2.473 | 1.244 | 0.000 |
| nearest-class | 0.000 | 0.225 | 0.933 | 0.000 | 0.008 | 0.066 | 0.005 | 1.874 | 1.050 | 0.000 |
| finetune-stream | 0.542±0.061 | 0.753 | 0.850 | 0.872 | 0.572 | 0.799 | 0.626 | 1.952 | 1.101 | 0.000 |

**Our headline ColorMNIST number moves from F1 0.498±0.030 to 0.605±0.043**, and joint accuracy from
roughly 0.31 to 0.539±0.025, entirely from calibrating a knob that had been left at its default.
Backgrounds go 0.82 → 0.911.  Nothing about the method changed.

Three further readings:

- **Vendi confirms the coverage story without a reference set.**  Ours is 1.942 against real data's
  1.901 -- we match the spread of the class -- while `poe-guided` is at 1.075, i.e. barely more than
  one effective mode, which is what recall 0.001 means stated a different way.
- **Nothing is memorizing.**  MOSAIC's copying test is 0.000 for every arm, so no method here is
  scoring by retrieving a training image.
- **`finetune-stream` still matches us** (F1 0.626 vs 0.605), which is §5n unchanged: on ColorMNIST a
  factored concept's semantics live in its private embedding, which no later concept can write to, so
  this row is preserved by isolation of the conditioning rather than by protecting the adapter.
  Forgetting is measured in n5, where the adapter carries the content.

## 12. The pretrained backbone (N25): what unblocked it

N15 left this open with a diagnosis — conditioning a 113.7M-parameter unconditional model through a
single additive vector on its time embedding does not move it. Giving the bank real capacity turned
out to be necessary but not sufficient, and the path to a working configuration went through three
distinct faults, each found by looking at samples rather than at numbers.

### 12a. Preservation transfers immediately

`tests/test_pretrained_bank.py`, on **google/ddpm-celebahq-256** (113.7M params) and
**google/ddpm-cifar10-32** (35.7M):

| assertion | result |
|---|---|
| injecting the bank changes the pretrained model | **0.000e+00** |
| a freshly registered concept is a no-op | **0.000e+00** |
| training a second concept moves the first | **0.000e+00** |

The preservation mechanism is structural, so it carries to a model we did not train without
